# Verified sources ledger

Every figure that reaches a page passes through here first. Read this before
researching anything. If a figure is logged, dated within 12 months and
carries two passing checks, reuse the exact citation rather than researching
it again. The ledger is what stops the same number being researched
forty-eight times and cited three different ways.

Seeded from Part 7 of `editorial/SEARCH-SPEC.md` on
2026-09-09. **Every seeded row still needs its two check dates before it can
be cited.** A seeded row with empty check columns is a research lead, not a
publishable figure.

## How to read a row

| Column | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Confidence | primary, triangulated, single-source, or market claim |
| Check 1 | fetched the URL, confirmed figure, unit, period and date on the page |
| Check 2 | re-fetched before the draft shipped, still says the same thing |
| Attribution | the exact wording that goes on the page. Never names a company for a market figure |

## hubStudio delivery figures

Published on the site already. Any of these on a page must carry the method:
what is counted, over what sample, across what period. A figure without its
method does not go on the page.

| Figure | Method still needed | Used in |
|---|---|---|
| Internal approval on AI assets moved from 22 percent to 78 percent | What counts as an approval, who approves, sample size, period | 29, 47 |
| 70/30 generated-to-shot mix on ecommerce | Sample of engagements, period | 12, 27 |
| 20/80 shoot-to-generate on variants | Sample of engagements, period | 27 |
| Custom model training 3 to 6 weeks | Observed range across how many engagements | 29 |
| Output scaled 7x | Baseline, period, what is counted as output | proof pages |
| 90-day file retention | Policy, not a legal requirement. State it as policy | 24, 28 |
| 48-hour binding proposal, 3 revision rounds | Policy | 41 |
| 35+ markets, 47 languages with lip sync | What counts as a market served | 43 |

Case study figures: HiSense live on a self-serve platform in 8 weeks,
thousands of assets per week. Premium SUV, 70 percent budget cut, 3 weeks
against 6 months, 15 markets, 20+ environments, zero vehicles shipped.
Mexicash, 5 platforms in 5 days. 1834 Gin, 46-second film, 3 weeks from zero
assets. iFlytek AnyPin, 22 cuts, 3 techniques. L'infuseur, 2 weeks, 3
channels. Any client figure not already public needs sign-off before it is
used.

## Platform specs already on the site

`/services/design/ecommerce` is the only page on the site carrying hard
platform numbers: Amazon RGB 255 mains (printed as 2,000px until 2026-10-08), Tmall 800x800 mains,
750x1,000 verticals, white slot under 300KB, titles inside 200 characters.
These need re-verification against the backend before they are repeated on a
spec page, because they are the numbers the spec cluster is supposed to
supersede. Brief 31 (2026-09-14) checked the Amazon figures against Amazon's
own help articles: RGB 255 matches; "2,000 pixels for zoom" does not appear on
any Amazon page read (1,000 or more enables zoom); the page's "fifteen to
thirty seconds for Amazon" video line conflicts with the Shoppable video
guide (1 to 12 minutes). Closed 2026-10-08 (briefs 31 and 51): the page now
reads "1,000 pixels or more on the longest side so zoom works" and "one to
twelve minutes at 16:9 or 9:16 for Amazon shoppable video".

## Do not publish without primary confirmation

Hard blocks. Publishing any of these from a secondary source is the single
most damaging thing this system can do.

1. **Tmall file size cap and product fill ratio.** Three Chinese sources
   conflict: 500KB against 3MB, and 60 percent against 70 to 80 percent fill.
   None distinguishes Tmall Global from domestic, and none accounts for
   category variation. Publish the range and the conflict, or capture it.
2. **Allocation of AI-labeling duties** between brand, agency and tool vendor
   under the CAC Measures. Law-firm interpretation, not a regulator statement.
   Write "generally treated as", never "requires".
3. **Penalties under the CAC Measures.** Not specified as a new schedule.
   Enforcement runs through existing instruments and is still described as
   unclear. Do not publish a fine figure.

## Digital replica contracts and personality rights (added 2026-09-09, brief 26)

Every row below was read on a primary surface: the state's own code display,
the legislature's own statute page, the regulator's own instrument page, or the
agreement PDF published by the bargaining body itself. **This upgrades the
union commercials row above.** That row records the union PDFs returning 403 to
automated and direct fetch, so it was triangulated through law firm analyses.
The same agreements are published by the advertiser and agency bargaining body
at jointpolicycommittee.org and downloaded cleanly there on 2026-09-09. Use
that host for any future union agreement claim.

| Figure or claim | Attribution to use | Source | Collected | Confidence | Check 1 | Check 2 | Used in |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Labor Code 927 is a three-condition unenforceability test, not a requirement: the provision must let a replica stand in for in-person work, AND lack a reasonably specific description of intended uses, AND the person must have been unrepresented by negotiating counsel or by a union whose collective agreement expressly addresses digital replicas. Applies only to a new performance fixed on or after 1 January 2025 | the code section, by number | leginfo.legislature.ca.gov, LAB 927 (a)(1), (a)(2)(A), (a)(2)(B), (a)(3)(A), (a)(3)(B), (b); added by Stats. 2024, Ch. 259 | 2026-09-09 | primary | 2026-09-09 | | 26 |
| No California decision applying section 927 was located. The only authoritative gloss on "reasonably specific" is the bargaining parties' own worked example inside their 2025 agreement | negative finding, stated as such | Searched 2026-09-09, none found | 2026-09-09 | primary (negative finding) | 2026-09-09 | | 26 |
| 2025 commercials agreement made 8 May 2025, three year term from 1 April 2025 to 31 March 2028. Digital replica defined as a computer program made in whole or part from a performer's voice, image or performance that can independently generate new performances not previously rendered | "the 2025 commercials agreement between the performers' union and the advertiser and agency bargaining body" | jointpolicycommittee.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/05/2025-Commercials-Contract-MOA.pdf, Section XX.A | 2025-05-08 | primary | 2026-09-09 | | 26 |
| Consent mechanics: 48 hours notice before the capture performance, 24 hours where engaged inside that window; casting notice must say a replica will be created when known; good-faith refusal at the time of performance is paid at the greater of negotiated rate or minimum | the agreement, by section | Same PDF, Section XX.C.1 | 2025-05-08 | primary | 2026-09-09 | | 26 |
| Valid consent needs a reasonably specific description, clear and conspicuous, via the Digital Replica Rider or a separate signed writing; a use beyond the description needs additional consent and a NEW description | the agreement, by section | Same PDF, Section XX.C.2 | 2025-05-08 | primary | 2026-09-09 | | 26 |
| Forward consent boundary: same advertiser and product line, production commencing inside the original commercial's maximum period of use, each new commercial carrying the same maximum period but in no event less than six months. Generating a performance for an unengaged commercial constitutes engagement | the agreement, by section | Same PDF, Sections XX.C.1.c and XX.C.1.d | 2025-05-08 | primary | 2026-09-09 | | 26 |
| Compensation: creation time is work time; generating a performance the person would otherwise have given in person pays 1.5 scale session fees per commercial, one session fee applicable to use fees; use and holding fees paid as if performed in person. **The multiple is publishable, the underlying session fee amount is not** | the agreement, by section, multiple only | Same PDF, Sections XX.D.1 to D.3 | 2025-05-08 | primary | 2026-09-09 | | 26 |
| Retention: during the commercial's maximum period of use; longer only by written bargained consent; 24 months from consent where no commercial was made, extendable only in further 24 month periods | the agreement, by section | Same PDF, Section XX.D.6.b | 2025-05-08 | primary | 2026-09-09 | | 26 |
| Deletion: absent consent to retain, permanent and irrevocable deletion of all copies with written certification to the performer within 90 days. Commercials already produced need not be destroyed | the agreement, by section | Same PDF, Section XX.D.7 | 2025-05-08 | primary | 2026-09-09 | | 26 |
| Training: producer may not use, or authorize another to use, the results and proceeds of services under the contracts to train generative AI systems without union consent | the agreement, by section | Same PDF, Section XX.1.A.4 | 2025-05-08 | primary | 2026-09-09 | | 26 |
| Synthetic performer definition, and the prompting rule: building one using an identified performer's name, image, photograph or recording in the prompt requires that person's consent and bargained payment at not less than the minimum session fee then in effect | the agreement, by section | Same PDF, Sections XX.1.A.1 and XX.1.A.3 | 2025-05-08 | primary | 2026-09-09 | | 26 |

## Tmall image specification, modal values (added 2026-09-10, brief 01)

Published under deviation 7 in `editorial/CLAUDE.md`, decided by the site owner
2026-09-10. **Every row below is a modal value, not a primary reading.** The
count is the evidence. Source pool: 18 independent domains carrying at least one
numeric Tmall or Taobao image figure, 16 Chinese-language and 2
English-language, collected 2026-09-10, Chinese web first. One domain counts
once however many pages it publishes. Full working in
`research/tmall-product-image-requirements.md`.

**Attribution form for every row:** "the most commonly published figure,
collected across 18 independent sources in Chinese and English on 10 September
2026, with N of 18 agreeing". Never "Tmall requires", never "the rule centre
states", never "verified".

| Figure | Modal value | Range across sources | Sources agreeing | Collected | Confidence | Check 1 | Check 2 | Used in |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Main image aspect ratio | 1:1 square, 3:4 available by category | 1:1 default, 3:4 held in about ten categories | 12 of 18 | 2026-09-10 | consensus, modal not primary | 2026-09-10 | | 01 |
| Main image dimensions | 800 by 800 pixels | 700 by 700 to 1440 by 1440 | 14 of 18 | 2026-09-10 | consensus, modal not primary | 2026-09-10 | | 01 |
| Main image recommended above standard | 1000 by 1000 pixels | 1000 by 1000 to 1500 by 1500 | 5 of 18 | 2026-09-10 | contested | 2026-09-10 | | 01 |
| The 1440 upgrade | 1440 by 1440 square, 1440 by 1920 vertical, 800 by 800 still displays | Opened 2025, favored since, not a floor | 3 of 18 | 2026-09-10 | contested | 2026-09-10 | | 01 |
| Main image count | 5 maximum | 5 on every domestic source giving a number; minimum required 3 on 2 sources | 9 of 18 | 2026-09-10 | consensus, modal not primary | 2026-09-10 | | 01 |
| File weight, platform cap | 3MB per image | 1MB for 3:4 verticals on one source, 10MB on one English outlier | 6 of 18 | 2026-09-10 | consensus, modal not primary | 2026-09-10 | | 01 |
| File weight, load-speed recommendation | Under 500KB | 200KB to 500KB | 3 of 18 | 2026-09-10 | contested, always labeled a recommendation not a cap | 2026-09-10 | | 01 |
| White-background slot file weight | Under 300KB, 38KB floor on 3 sources | Under 300KB, or loosely a few hundred KB | 4 of 18 | 2026-09-10 | consensus among sources treating the slot separately | 2026-09-10 | | 01 |
| Accepted formats | JPG and PNG | JPG universal, PNG on 4, GIF on 1, BMP on 1 | 8 of 18 JPG, 4 of 18 PNG | 2026-09-10 | consensus for JPG, contested beyond | 2026-09-10 | | 01 |
| White-background slot position | Fifth main image | Fifth in general categories, second in food and appliances | 8 of 18 | 2026-09-10 | consensus general, contested by category | 2026-09-10 | | 01 |

| Product fill, white-background slot | 70 to 80 percent | 60 to 80 percent, with 3 sources at a flat about 70 percent | 4 of 18 for the band, 3 more inside it | 2026-09-10 | contested for the figure, consensus for the 60 to 80 band | 2026-09-10 | | 01 |

| Detail page per-slice weight and height | Under 500KB, 1500 pixels tall | 200KB to 3MB, 1500 to 2000 pixels | 2 of 18 each | 2026-09-10 | contested | 2026-09-10 | | 01 |
| Listing video weight and format | 50MB, MP4 with H.264 | 50MB on all 3 that give one, MOV accepted on 1 | 3 of 18 | 2026-09-10 | consensus among the 3 that address video | 2026-09-10 | | 01 |

**Hard block 1 is resolved as a category error, not a conflict.** The ledger
recorded 500KB against 3MB as rival readings of one number. They are not. Every
source publishing both treats 3MB as the platform cap and 500KB as a load-speed
recommendation. The fill-ratio half of that block stands: 60 to 80 percent
remains a genuine spread with no single mode inside it beyond the 70 to 80 band.

reading. Collection: 15 independent domains carrying white-background image
content, 14 Chinese-language and 1 English-language, collected 2026-09-10, 10
read in full and 5 through search extracts only. Counts are domains, not pages.

## Douyin paid ad creative specs, modal values (added 2026-09-10, brief 06)

reading. Collection: 17 sources carrying at least one Douyin paid-ad spec value,
16 Chinese-language and 1 English-language, collected 2026-09-10, counted by
independent domain. One TikTok help page was read and excluded from every
Douyin count. The only platform-hosted spec document reachable is a
fourth-quarter 2020 Douyin rate card that expired 2020-12-31 by its own terms and
defers to an online sheet that is not public: it counts once, flagged by age.

advertiser backend. Full working in `research/douyin-ad-creative-specs-by-format.md`.

### Corrections to existing ledger rows (2026-09-17, brief 33)

| Row | Correction |
|---|---|
| Published studio per-asset rate cards, brief 11 | **Relabel.** The row is marked triangulated as a whole. Only $39 to $50 is on two cards. The $95 lifestyle, $93 clip, $89 UGC, $199 pro edit and $49 add-on are each single-source. Article 33 labels them correctly; briefs 11 and 12 inherited the looser label |
| Chinese ecommerce photography rate card, brief 13 | **Relabel.** "five-style minimum, 6 to 10 images a style" reads as two terms of the card. The 17 September re-read shows the five-set minimum is binding but the 6 to 10 images figure is a general-practice note in the page's own FAQ. Any derivation multiplying the two is withdrawn |
| Ecommerce retouch floor $0.25 to $1.20 | The top of the band read $1.19 on 2026-09-17, for image masking and multi-clipping. Two specialty operations sit above the band at $2.99 and $3.99 and always did. "Roughly $0.25 to $1.20" still holds as written |
| Chinese detail-page conflict, brief 13 | **Correction.** "None of them states what a set contains" is no longer accurate. The 2026 page defines a set as twenty pieces: main images, the detail-page cover, scene images and detail shots. Dividing gives 75 to 250 RMB an asset, which overlaps the 2023 page's 200 to 300 RMB, so most of the order-of-magnitude gap is a unit difference |
| Translation band, $0.08 to $0.18 a source word | **Ledger defect.** The source is recorded as "multiple vendor pricing pages collected 2026-09-09, no company named" with no URL list, so check 2 cannot be run on it. The row needs its URLs recorded before the next cost article cites it |

### Check 2 corrections, 2026-09-21

| Row | What the re-fetch found | What was done |
|---|---|---|
| CAC labeling Measures, the row used by briefs 13, 23, 24 and 26 | **RAISED AND THEN RETRACTED THE SAME DAY. NO ACTION IS NEEDED ON ANY PUBLISHED ARTICLE.** A first re-fetch of cac.gov.cn/2025-03/14/c_1743654685899683.htm appeared not to reproduce the three metadata field names or the export-retention line, and a flag was raised against briefs 13, 23, 24 and 26. That was wrong. The cac.gov.cn URL is the release notice, not the instrument text, so the article text was never on the page being fetched. Read at press.nppa.gov.cn/zcfg/202606/t20260609_993253.html, a surface run by one of the four issuing bodies, Article 5 carries all three fields verbatim: 生成合成内容属性信息, 服务提供者名称或者编码, 内容编号. Article 4's final paragraph carries the export duty verbatim: 服务提供者提供生成合成内容下载、复制、导出等功能时，应当确保文件中含有满足要求的显式标识 | The ledger row stands as written and is confirmed primary. **Lesson worth keeping: for a Chinese instrument, a cac.gov.cn 通知 page is the announcement and often will not serve the article text. Cite an issuing body's own 政策法规 page for article-level claims. Use press.nppa.gov.cn/zcfg/202606/t20260609_993253.html as the article-text surface for these Measures** |
| US Copyright Office registration guidance, 88 FR 16190 | Passed. Returned the verbatim: "copyright can protect only material that is the product of human creativity", a duty to "disclose the inclusion of AI-generated content in a work submitted for registration", and that more than de minimis AI content "should be explicitly excluded from the application". The ledger's "enter it in Material Excluded" wording is not on that page | Brief 35's blockquote rewritten to the source's own words. The "Material Excluded" phrasing is not used |
| 2025 commercials agreement, training clause | Passed. PDF re-downloaded and text-extracted. Verbatim: "Producer may not use or authorize any other party to use the results and proceeds of services rendered under the Commercials Contracts to train GAI systems without consent of the Union." The section reads as XX.1, Training Data, item 4. The ledger's XX.1.A.4 citation was not reproducible from the extraction | Brief 35 quotes the verbatim and cites Section XX.1, Training Data |
| UK advertiser trade body hourly rate benchmarks | Passed. Still published: "The 2025 Agency Hourly Rates Benchmarks are available to members who contribute to the benchmarking exercise", data "only published to members in an aggregated form with no reference to the individual contributors" | Check 2 date recorded. Row unchanged |
| UK advertising regulator AI and deepfakes guidance | Passed. "The Code is media-neutral, meaning the rules apply regardless of how the content was created" and "If an AI tool produces something problematic, the advertiser remains responsible for any output used in their ads." Page dated 11 Jun 2026 | Check 2 date recorded. Row unchanged |

| California Labor Code 927 and AB 2602 | Ledger, brief 26 | They govern a performer's digital replica, not an agency-to-supplier production contract. Category error if stretched |
| "60 to 80 percent cost reduction versus traditional production" | hubStudio's own agencies page and partner program page | First-party cost-saving claim with no published method. The R5 first-party exception reaches the delivery figures in this ledger and does not reach this one |
| Partner program commission percentages, residual percentages and gross-margin advancement thresholds | src/pages/partner-program.astro | hubStudio commercial terms. Caught by the no-rate rule and by brief 35's explicit ban on publishing a hubStudio margin. The partner program is named as a separate referral route with no figure attached |
| The 22 to 78 percent approval-rate figure | src/pages/partner-program.astro | Banned by brief 35 |
| GDPR fine ceilings quoted on a ranking page | A vendor blog, 2026-06-02 | Correct as a headline and irrelevant to the clause structure. Quoting a penalty ceiling to make a contract point is scare framing |
| Any penalty figure under the CAC labeling Measures | Standing hard block 3 | Not specified as a new schedule |

### The 2026 model roster: maker claims, commercial terms, dated leaderboard readings (added 2026-09-10, brief 40)

Planning decision, brief 40, 2026-09-10: generation models and their makers are
named as tools, because site pages already name them. Leaderboards are
attributed by category with the read date and method, never by name. Apps,
studios, agencies, automation platforms and resellers built on models are never
named. Every maker capability is a maker claim. Full working in
`research/the-2026-model-roster.md`.
